L1602: ## 3) Exposure-safety Relationships
L1603: There was a shallow E-R relationship for myalgia any grade and ripretinib Ctrough as well as palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia (PPES) any grade and ripretinib Ctrough. However, graphical quartile analyses do not suggest that combined ripretinib and DP-5439 Cmin are associated with evaluated adverse events of any grade (Figure 14). Moreover, the rate of grade 3+ events for PPES syndrome, hypertension, myalgia, diarrhea and hyperbilirubinemia or increased blood bilirubin are very low and do not appear to be associated with combined ripretinib and DP-5439 Cmin (Figure 15). The baseline covariates across four exposure quartiles in the exposure-safety analyses appeared to be balanced (Table 30).

L1611: ## 19.3.3 KIT or PDGFRA mutations and treatment response
L1612: In Study DCC-2618-03-001, KIT and PDGFRA mutation status was retrospectively assessed by a central lab in tumor samples (archived or fresh) of 128 patients (112 evaluable; 73 in the ripretinib arm and 39 in the placebo arm) using a next generation sequencing (NGS)-based assay. In Study DCC-2618-01-001, KIT and PDGFRA mutation information was available for 83 patients from patient's medical history. Over 80% of patients had KIT mutations in both studies, mostly involving exon 11. The number of ripretinib-treated patients and responses (CR or PR) in mutation-defined subgroups is summarized in Table 31. Among patients with KIT mutations in exon 11 (alone or combined with secondary mutations), the ORR was 16.7% (8/47) in Study DCC-2618-03-001 and 8.6% (5/58) in Study DCC-2618-01-001. No objective responses were observed among patients with KIT exon 9 mutations (alone or combined with mutations in KIT exon 17) or in patients with GIST negative for PDGFRA or KIT mutations.

L1638: Flowchart illustrating the DCC-2618-03-001 Study Schematic.
Randomization (2:1, n=120):
Ripretinib 150 mg QD (n=80, 28-day cycles)
Placebo (n=40, 28-day cycles)
Stratification (Prior treatments):
3 vs ≥4
ECOG PS: 0 vs 1 or 2
Follow-up (Disease progression by blinded independent central review/unblinding):
Ripretinib Arm (n=80):
Dose escalate to 150 mg BID
OR Continue on same dose
OR Discontinue study treatment
Placebo Arm (n=40):
Cross over to ripretinib 150 mg QD
OR Discontinue study treatment
Post-Cross-over/Discontinuation (Disease progression):
Dose escalate to 150 mg BID
OR Continue on same dose
OR Discontinue study treatment

L1645: Figure 8 displays two side-by-side Visual Predictive Checks (VPCs) of Ripretinib concentration versus time after the previous dose, stratified by clinical studies DCC-2618-01-001 (left) and DCC-2618-03-001 (right).
The Y-axis represents Ripretinib Conc. (ng/mL), ranging from 0 to 3000. The X-axis represents Time After Previous Dose (Hrs), ranging from 0 to 25.
Observed data points are shown as black dots. Predicted concentration profiles are overlaid as solid red lines, with dashed red lines indicating the upper and lower bounds of the prediction interval.
Both plots show a rapid increase in concentration immediately after dosing (around 0-5 hours), followed by a decline. The concentration generally remains above 1000 ng/mL for the first 24 hours in both studies. The predicted profiles show a peak concentration around 5-10 hours, followed by a steady decline.
Study DCC-2618-01-001 shows a predicted peak concentration around 1500 ng/mL. Study DCC-2618-03-001 shows a predicted peak concentration around 2000 ng/mL. The observed data points generally fall within the predicted bounds, although there are some outliers, particularly at higher concentrations (above 2500 ng/mL) and lower concentrations (below 500 ng/mL).
L1646: Figure 9 displays two Visual Predictive Checks (VPCs) for DP-5439 concentration versus time after the previous dose, stratified by clinical studies: DCC-2618-01-001 (left panel) and DCC-2618-03-001 (right panel).

The plots show observed concentration data points (black dots) and predicted concentration intervals (shaded blue areas). The predicted intervals are defined by the 5th and 95th percentiles of the predicted distribution.
